That is what makes this worse than a false completeness claim: a false one is refuted by any counterexample within its stated domain, while a true one over an UNSTATED domain has no counterexample inside the domain it names, so the reader cannot separate `all cases of the argument` from `all cases of the decision`. SPECIMEN (gunbc#9725): five refusal arms of the bootstrap-lease admission were each shown going red under mutation, and `six arms of one argument is not the gate; the gate is the relation between two` was the correction. The repair was structural rather than another arm -- FUSE THE DOOR: the gate no longer accepts a pre-computed admission, it derives the admission itself from the envelope it is judging, so the mismatched pair has no constructor. RECOGNITION RULE: when a matrix is enumerated per argument, ask WHICH RELATIONS BETWEEN ARGUMENTS THE FUNCTION SIGNATURE ADMITS. If two parameters can be derived from different subjects, the product space -- not the union of the axes -- is the domain, and an axis-wise sweep is exhaustive over a projection of it. The tell is a decision function whose arguments are each independently derivable by a caller. THE NEIGHBOUR THIS IS NOT: [[check_subject_narrower_than_its_declared_claim]] is a subject mismatch that a reader can catch by comparing the claim to the check; here the claim and the check agree exactly, and the narrowing is in the DOMAIN both of them share. NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER, a capability and not an artifact: deriving a decision's dependent inputs INSIDE the decision rather than accepting them, wherever two parameters name the same subject -- which is the fused-door move generalized, and makes the off-diagonal unconstructible instead of merely unenumerated. RUNG FOUND AT: mechanically preventable, by a probe that had to be written to construct the state. CEILING: structurally impossible, and reached for this specimen.)
- **a subject defined by a CLOSURE with no enumerable membership cannot be protected in advance, only violated in arrears** (INVALID STATE: a receipt, cache key, or admission is pinned to a digest computed over a TRANSITIVE CLOSURE -- here the wet lane's `semantic_subject`, over the per-entry closure of 23 routed witness entries plus `v2.workflow.floor_wet_route`. The digest is a perfectly good detector and a useless protector: it reports a MISMATCH AFTER THE FACT rather than a MEMBERSHIP LIST BEFORE it. HARM: a two-hour dispatch completed and its receipt arrived uncommittable as `ReceiptSubjectDifferent`, because a commit landed inside the closure after the run's head. The lane that would break such a subject cannot decide for itself whether its diff intersects one, so the only available discipline is asking the holder afterwards -- which is not a discipline, it is a post-mortem. THE VISIBLE SURFACE IS NOT THE SUBJECT, and reporting it as one is the same defect in a different costume: the wet subject's visible surface is 19 files, while the resolver's own lines report 47 modules and 1180 resolved items for ONE entry, reaching transitively into `src/v2/std/` and `src/v2/compiler/`. A lane can invalidate the receipt while touching none of the 19 and doing nothing wrong. A list handed a completeness property by the mere fact that it is a list is the trap; the resolver's numbers are what refute it, which is why they are quoted rather than estimated. THE CONSEQUENCE FOR ANNOUNCEMENTS, recorded because it is the half that fools the announcer: a hold announced over an unenumerable subject is not protection, and the artifact recording that the announcement happened is indistinguishable from the protection existing. A hold nobody can check is WORSE than no hold, because its holder then treats the subject as protected. SCOPE, and it is wider than the specimen: EVERY subject-pinned receipt in this repository has this hole; the others have not noticed because none has yet burned a long run to it. RECOGNITION RULE: when a digest pins a subject, ask WHETHER ANYTHING PRINTS THE SUBJECT'S MEMBERSHIP. If the only instrument that knows the membership is the comparison itself, the subject is detectable and not defendable, and any fence described over it is a request rather than a constraint. NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER, a capability and not an artifact: a producer that PRINTS the closure membership for a routed entry, SUFFICIENT that a lane can decide FOR ITSELF, BEFORE LANDING, whether its diff intersects a live subject. THE POINTED PART, CORRECTED ONCE ALREADY AND THE CORRECTION IS THE WHOLE VALUE: the set is not merely computed and discarded, it is COMPUTED, HELD, AND RETURNED TO THE CALLER. In `v1_compiler.claim_batch` `resolve_timed`, the resolved graph is bound, its `modules` and `item_registry` are read for their LENGTHS to print the `[resolve]` line, and the whole graph is then handed back to the caller. Nothing is thrown away except THE NAMES, and only at the `eprintln!`. So the missing thing is an EMITTER over a set the process already holds and the caller already receives -- not an analysis, not a resolver change. THIS DISTINCTION IS THE ROW'S RUNG HONESTY AND 4b(2) TURNS ON IT: `nobody can determine this in advance` and `nobody has printed it` are different claims. The first is a permanent ceiling under which every future lane pays the same cost again; the second is an unbuilt next rung one emitter away. Filed as the former this row would sound honest and be wrong, and would foreclose its own repair -- which is this very class applied to our own tooling, a claim about a population made by an instrument that never enumerated it. RUNG FOUND AT: mitigatable, and the mitigation is a message. CEILING: mechanically preventable -- membership is decidable and computed, but a lane's intersection with a LIVE subject depends on which runs are in flight, which is external state observed at a boundary.)
- **a bound is never EVALUATED for a whole population, so the first unrelated edit that makes one of them run discovers the line as a merge block** (INVALID STATE: an identity that the ordinary floor never plans is subject to a ceiling that has therefore never been exercised against it. Specimen: `test.claim.witness_floor_workflow_consolidation_witness_test.w_RED_fabric_evidence_executes_without_gating` has ZERO planned rows on main -- checked in the `required-floor-claim-cost` artifact of two consecutive main runs, 0 of 3549 -- because it executes ONLY as a CHANGED witness. Its 500ms CPU line was first exercised by gunbc#9725, a change about the wet execution route, which touched `gunbc.witness_floor_workflow` and thereby made the witness changed; it refused at 504ms and 505ms on two heads. HARM: the discovering PR is not the causing PR in any sense its author can act on. The block arrives on a change that has nothing to do with cost, the author has no baseline to compare against because none was ever produced, and the only available dispositions are to enroll a debt row or to stall. THE NEIGHBOUR THIS IS NOT, and the distinction is the whole row: the NEAR-LINE family says a bound is MISPLACED -- measured over the whole corpus by the FLOOR-COST-500MS lane, 21 of the ~29 identities that can approach the line at all sit within +/-10% of it with nothing beyond 541ms, so the threshold was drawn through a cluster. This row says a bound was NEVER EVALUATED for a population at all. Different invalid state, different trigger, and the repairs diverge: a per-witness declared cost envelope fixes the misplaced line, and does NOT by itself cause the ceiling to be exercised for identities the ordinary floor does not plan. WHY IT IS STRUCTURALLY INVISIBLE TO THE OBVIOUS INSTRUMENT: a census that samples what the ordinary floor PLANS has this population outside its denominator BY CONSTRUCTION -- the cost lane's own survey could not see the class, and found it only when a lane that had tripped the line reported it. This is [[green_reported_over_a_population_the_instrument_does_not_own]] in the cost dimension: the survey was accurate about the rows it enumerated and silent about the rows it could not. RECOGNITION RULE: when a bound is declared per-identity but enforcement is gated on a SELECTION (changed, affected, sampled, scheduled), ask WHICH IDENTITIES THE SELECTION NEVER SELECTS ON THE MAINLINE. Those carry unevaluated bounds, and the population is discoverable in advance -- it is the declared identities minus the planned ones, both of which are already published per run. NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER, a capability and not an artifact: the ceiling is EXERCISED AND ITS RESULT PUBLISHED for every identity carrying one, including those the ordinary floor does not plan -- sufficient that a bound's first contact with an identity is a measurement someone chose to take, not a merge block on an unrelated change. Until then the class is discovered one PR at a time by whoever happens to edit the triggering authority. RUNG FOUND AT: mitigatable, and the mitigation is a debt row written after the fact. CEILING: mechanically preventable -- the unplanned population is a decidable set difference over data each run already emits.)
- **a subject digest taken over RAW FILE CONTENT makes annotations semantically load-bearing, which is the property DESIGN section 4c declares impossible** (INVALID STATE: a receipt, cache key or admission is pinned to a digest computed by `v1_compiler.resolved_graph_cache` `closure_content_digest`, which folds each source's `content` -- the authored bytes -- rather than anything the annotation-erased projection would produce. So ADDING, DELETING OR REFLOWING A COMMENT in a module inside such a subject moves the digest. 4c states the opposite in terms: annotation capture is disjoint from semantic occurrence allocation, and adding, deleting or moving an annotation cannot alter any semantic occurrence identity, semantic graph, resolution result, SEMANTIC HASH, or target-program bytes. HARM, MEASURED RATHER THAN IMAGINED: on gunbc#9725 the wet lane's semantic subject folds `v2.workflow.floor_wet_route`'s own closure content, a receipt from a 2h31m dispatch was pinned to that digest, and an operator-granted one-shot lease pinned the same value as `exact_semantic_subject_digest`. Repairing a one-word COMMENT in that module would have voided the grant and forced a re-dispatch plus a second operator grant. Three real defects were therefore repaired in the seed and left standing in the substrate authority, which is filed separately as `gunbc.guarantee_stall` `wet_route_model_lags_seed_stall`. The next occurrence will not be so visible: someone reflows a paragraph and silently invalidates a receipt with no diagnostic connecting the two. THE DISJUNCTION, because only one of these can be true and neither is currently written down. EITHER the closure content digest is NOT a semantic hash in 4c's sense -- in which case it must stop being read as one, and the distinction between a CONTENT identity (what bytes were on disk) and a SEMANTIC identity (what program they denote) has to be authored somewhere a reader meets before pinning anything to it. OR 4c's guarantee genuinely does not hold for this carrier, and 4c is overclaiming for every consumer that digests sources rather than the erased projection. RECOGNITION RULE: when a digest is described as a subject, a semantic subject, or a program identity, ask WHAT IT FOLDS. If the answer is file content, then comments, whitespace and formatting are inside it, and every guarantee stated about annotations being semantically inert is false of that digest -- while reading exactly like a guarantee about the program. The tell is a digest whose name says semantic and whose producer takes `content`. NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER, a capability and not an artifact: a subject digest folded over the ANNOTATION-ERASED projection that semantic passes already receive -- the same erasure 4c requires those passes to consume -- so that the digest is invariant under comment edits by construction and 4c's guarantee is true of it rather than merely stated near it. Failing that, the weaker form: the content digest is RENAMED to say it is a content identity and every pinning consumer is re-read against that meaning. RUNG FOUND AT: mitigatable, and the mitigation is that authors happen to know. CEILING: structurally impossible -- an erased-projection digest cannot move on an annotation edit, because the annotation is not in the input.)
- **a value and a summary of it -- a digest, a count, a view, an annotation -- placed side by side without a join can disagree silently, and the corroboration the reader believes they are getting was never computed** (INVALID STATE: a signature takes a value AND a summary of that value as two peer arguments -- `envelope` beside `envelope_digest`, `roster_identities` beside `roster_digest` -- with no arm relating them. The signature admits a digest of something OTHER than the value beside it; nothing refuses; and every downstream guarantee that cites the digest is then citing a summary of a different object, while reading exactly like a guarantee about the value. DISTINGUISHING FACTS: the two arguments are supplied by the same caller from the same site TODAY, so the pairing is held by lexical proximity rather than by anything the type system or a fold can see; the defect is constructible without touching a single caller, by supplying a correct digest of a different subject. EVIDENCE, TWO INSTANCES IN ONE MODULE, which is what makes it a class rather than a coincidence: `v2.workflow.floor_wet_route` `wet_route_gate_disposition_for_receipt` carries BOTH the envelope/digest pair and the roster-identities/roster-digest pair. The first was demonstrated live -- `v2.test.floor_wet_route` `wet_lease_admits_an_envelope_whose_content_the_digest_does_not_name` constructs an envelope whose content the pinned digest does not name, and the lease gate ADMITS. THE SAME DEFECT READ FROM THE OTHER END: agreement among derived views is vacuous when the views share one reader. A projection was nearly shipped with a row silently deleted because FOUR derived views agreed, all fed by a single reader -- one source feeding many views made agreement carry no information, exactly as a digest and its subject being independent lets them disagree unnoticed. THE THIRD FACE, AND THE ONE THAT SURVIVED REVIEW LONGEST: PROSE ADJACENCY IS NOT A JOIN EITHER. `wet_route_identity_rows_block_with_publication` carried an annotation stating that EXACTLY TWO per-identity classes are waived under a valid publication transaction, three lines above a body that returned false unconditionally and waived FOUR -- so `no_verdict`, the ABSENCE of a verdict, was converted into a green merge path. The prose and the code disagreed by a factor of two IN THE SAME FUNCTION and FOUR APPROVALS READ PAST IT. An annotation is not checked against the body it sits above by anything: it READS as documentation and FUNCTIONS as an assertion, and by DESIGN section 4c no `Accepted` program can read one, so nothing in the toolchain will ever disagree with it. Provider count is not attention -- four approvals is not four readings of that function. AND FIELD ADJACENCY IS NOT A JOIN EITHER, which is the same claim one scale down: in `v1_compiler.bin.claim_batch`'s two pre-execution refusal arms the annotation two lines above `WetReceiptIdentityRow` was RIGHT, and the neighbouring fields `observed_entry_rel` and `observed_function` were RIGHT -- Optional, and correctly None on exactly those arms -- and `wall_ms`, two fields later in the SAME STRUCT LITERAL, fabricates a 0 for a duration nothing measured. PROXIMITY TO A CORRECT THING IS NOT CORRECTNESS: a reader scanning that literal gets three signals of care and one fabrication, with nothing in the text distinguishing them. And 0 is the WORST available fabricated value for that field rather than a neutral placeholder, because it is THE FASTEST OBSERVABLE EXECUTION -- so a consumer that averages, thresholds or ranks on wall_ms without first branching on the outcome wire reads the ABSENCE of a measurement as the BEST measurement in the corpus. An anomalous-looking fabrication is found by whoever first sorts the column; this one sorts to the top and is found by nobody. All three faces are one defect: A JOIN THAT WAS NEVER MADE, so the corroboration a reader believes they are getting was never computed. RECOGNITION RULE: whenever a parameter list contains a value and a summary, hash, count, digest or identity OF that value, ask what refuses when they disagree. If the answer is nothing, the summary is unverified input wearing the costume of a derived fact. The mirror question for agreement: when N sources agree, ask how many INDEPENDENT reads produced them; if the answer is one, the agreement is a copy agreeing with itself. THE REPAIR, already built once and therefore not speculative: DERIVE THE DIGEST FROM THE VALUE AT THE POINT THE VALUE IS OBTAINED, NEVER ACCEPT IT AS A PEER PARAMETER. The seed`s `read_wet_receipt_envelope` now returns the decoded envelope and the digest of the bytes it decoded FROM, in one call, so the two cannot name different objects; its control `carried_digest_names_the_bytes_the_envelope_was_decoded_from` rewrites the file between reads and asserts the carried digest still names its own bytes. RUNG FOUND AT: mitigatable -- a comment and a caller convention. CEILING: structurally impossible, because a digest produced by the same operation that produces the value has no constructor that lets it name a different one. NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER, a capability: a carrier type that pairs a value with a digest DERIVED at construction, so no signature anywhere can accept the two separately -- the `.dag` instances named in `gunbc.guarantee_stall` `wet_route_model_lags_seed_stall` are the population that trigger retires.)
- **a written rule is how a LATER READER learns what happened; it is not how a PRESENT AUTHOR is stopped, so a ledger that grows without changing what is easy to type buys documentation and no prevention** (INVALID STATE: a failure class is answered by APPENDING A ROW -- to this roster, to a stall roster, to an annotation -- and the row is then counted as the remedy. The class recurs anyway, because at the moment of authoring the dangerous form is still the shortest thing to type and nothing costs more when it is chosen. RECOGNITION RULE, which is the whole row: after filing, ask WHAT IS NOW HARDER TO DO THAN IT WAS BEFORE. If the honest answer is nothing, the filing was a RECORD and not a REPAIR, the class rung did not move, and it must be reported as UNREMEDIED rather than as covered. WHY IT IS NOT MERELY WEAK BUT ACTIVELY MISLEADING: a filed class gets CITED AS COVERAGE, so a reviewer meeting it reads it as handled and the filing converts an open deficit into a closed-looking one while leaving the deficit exactly where it was -- a DESIGN section 4b(1) rung wearing a 4b(2) costume, which is this ledger own version of rung inflation. An unrepaired filing is therefore WORSE than no filing, not equal to it. WHY THE RECALL DOES NOT FIRE: a rule filed under its SUBJECT MATTER is retrieved by thinking about that subject matter, and an author executing a step attends to THE GOAL, not the class of the step. Nothing in `retire the superseded poller` cues `I am about to type a pattern that can match myself`. THE TEST RUN ON A REAL POPULATION THE SAME NIGHT IT WAS WRITTEN, AND IT CAME BACK MOSTLY NEGATIVE: across ten class-filings required in one day, the identity-join rows made nothing harder (`sort -u` is exactly as easy to type as `sort`); the prose-adjacency, field-adjacency and digest-beside-subject rows made nothing harder (no lens refuses a digest passed beside its subject, and that signature is still the natural one to write); the empty-reading rows made nothing harder (the short read is still shorter than the head-filtered one, which is precisely why four people reached for it). ONE of the ten changed what is easy, and IT WAS NOT A FILING: a schema hazard was answered by computing the affected population and commenting on each of the five pull requests about to hit it, delivering friction to five named authors rather than prose to a ledger. A second was initially scored as a climb and the correction is the sharper result: the carrier ALREADY HAD THE RIGHT SHAPE and nothing about it was built that day; what the day produced was the DISCOVERY that a wrong thing was writable beside it, because an author wrote a fabricated previous-rung into a drop row, A REGEN RAN GREEN OVER IT, and a reader happened to look at the field. NO MECHANISM WAS WATCHING. Corrected score: ONE push-friction instance that expires when five pull requests land, ZERO climbs, ONE discovery -- and the discovery is the least repeatable item in the list, since the next occurrence is written where nobody is reading and the green regen is identical. A LUCKY READ IS NOT COVERAGE. THE TWO KINDS OF FRICTION ARE NOT INTERCHANGEABLE AND ONLY ONE COMPOUNDS: PUSH friction is delivered to named authors at the moment of impact and EXPIRES when their changes land; PULL-PROOF friction removes the constructor, so nothing needs delivering to anyone ever again. A day producing one of each must not report two. THE CONCLUSION THE AUDIT FORCES, STRONGER THAN THIS ROW STARTED: retrieval-by-recall is not a mechanism that becomes adequate at a smaller ledger size -- IT IS THE WRONG MECHANISM AT ANY SIZE, because the retrieval cue never arrives regardless of how few rows there are. Every row real deliverable is THE FRICTION, and the prose is its RECEIPT rather than its product. RUNG FOUND AT: mitigatable, and this row is an instance of what it describes, which is why it carries a trigger rather than resting on having been written. CEILING: structurally impossible for any class whose dangerous form can be removed from the vocabulary entirely -- an unwritable form needs no rule. NEXT-RUNG TRIGGER, a capability and not an artifact: for each filed class, a named ARTEFACT OF FRICTION -- a lens that refuses the shape, a carrier with no unsafe constructor, a wrapper shorter to invoke than the raw form -- so that a filing arrives WITH a change in what is easy, and the obligation is symmetric: whoever ASKS for a row owes the friction artefact or the plain sentence that nothing got harder and the class stands UNREMEDIED.)
- **a gate that admits when its required evidence has not reported, because the admission arm keys on OBSERVED FAILURE rather than on OBSERVED SUCCESS.** INVALID STATE: a required check exists, is correctly configured, and is consulted -- and the consulting arm asks `has anything reported a failure` where the guarantee needs `has everything reported a success`. Between those two questions sits the state where nothing has reported at all, and it is admitted. HARM, AND THE REASON THE CLASS SURVIVES REVIEW: every artifact reads as present and correct. The gate is enumerated in the required roster, it is active, it blocks a reported failure exactly as advertised, and a reader auditing the configuration finds no drift -- because there is none. The defect is in the QUANTIFIER, and a quantifier has no configuration surface to inspect. DISTINGUISHING FACT, AND IT IS THE RACE RATHER THAN ANY FIELD: the class is only reachable inside the window between the evidence being DEMANDED and the evidence ARRIVING, so it is invisible to any observation taken after that window closes -- afterwards the check has reported, the record looks ordinary, and only the ORDER of two timestamps distinguishes an admitted merge from a blocked one. RECOGNITION RULE: for every gate, find the arm that admits and read what it is quantified over. If it enumerates the evidence that HAS arrived and asks a property of each, the empty enumeration admits. If it enumerates the evidence that is REQUIRED and asks each for a success, it refuses. The two spellings are one word apart and behave identically on every input except the one the class lives in. DISTINCT FROM `absorbing_fallback`: that arm WIDENS when it cannot compute an answer and is at least loud about doing work; this arm NARROWS to the empty set and is silent, because a fold over nothing returns its seed and nobody wrote the seed down as a decision. **SPECIMEN (eager-bear-107, gunbc#10236, 2026-09-03), and it landed a break rather than merely permitting one.** The pull request pushed head d8cd3034 at 19:49:33Z, GitHub created its `witnesses` run at 19:49:40Z, and the merge completed at 19:49:43Z -- three seconds after run creation, before any job of it reported. That run then concluded with `required-witnesses-build` and `required-witnesses-floor` both FAILURE, and the head it certified nothing about landed on main as cfe19ea7, adding two duplicate declarations to THIS MODULE that refused main at resolve until a repair pull request landed. WHICH LANE CAUGHT IT IS PART OF THE FILING, because the two main flakes are separable in this evidence and a reader who conflates them loses that: on the post-merge main run at cfe19ea7 the duplicates reddened `required-witnesses-build` while `required-witnesses-floor` was GREEN, so the break is not the cost class this repository's other main flake belongs to and cannot be waved away as more floor variance. THE DETECTING LANE WORKED AND WAS TWENTY MINUTES LATE, which is the shape of this class rather than an aggravating detail: `required-witnesses-build` refused the duplicates correctly -- `generated-artifact CarrierRefused cause=resolve`, the authority declining to answer -- and its job completed at 20:04:14Z against a merge at 19:49:43Z, an unadjudicated interval of twenty minutes and thirty-one seconds. NOTHING WAS MISROUTED AND NO LANE WAS MISSING. **A RETRACTION IS RECORDED HERE BECAUSE THE MISREADING IS ITSELF AN INSTANCE OF A ROSTERED CLASS.** An earlier revision of this row said the catcher was `heal-generated-artifacts` and that it was event-conditional, on the evidence that heal FAILED on the pull-request run and was SKIPPED on the main run of identical content. The job graph refutes it: heal died at step 2, `Checkout triggering branch head`, at 19:52:01Z, with `Regenerate every committed generated artifact` SKIPPED -- the regenerator never ran, so heal detected nothing, and it failed because the merge had deleted the branch head it wanted three seconds after the run was created. A downstream consequence of the merge was read as a detection of the defect. That is `green_reported_over_a_population_the_instrument_does_not_own` inverted -- a RED reported over a population the instrument never reached -- and the recognition rule transfers exactly: A JOB'S CONCLUSION IS NOT EVIDENCE ABOUT THE SUBJECT IT IS NAMED FOR UNTIL ITS STEP RECORD SHOWS IT REACHED THE STEP THAT JUDGES. It survived four readers passing a narrative and was caught by one who read the job graph. Ruleset 16178731 carried zero divergence from `gunbc.repo_ruleset` desired state throughout. Over the forty most recently merged pull requests, six had no `witnesses` run that completed successfully on their own head before `merged_at`. **THE SAME SHAPE IN THIS REPOSITORY'S OWN MODEL, which is what makes it a class and not an incident:** `gunbc.merge_lifecycle` `merge_enabled` folded `policy_admits` over the receipts found for a head from `init: false`, so the absent-receipt verdict was a caller-local constant standing in for a policy fact. There the seed was the SAFE answer and the harm was the mirror image -- the live behavior became unrepresentable, no witness could go red on it, and the module named a policy stronger than the live one as live for two months. RUNG FOUND AT: mitigatable, and only by human habit. CEILING: structurally guaranteed -- the merge queue construction makes the queued ref's run a precondition of landing rather than a fact observed beside it. NEXT TRIGGER: `gunbc.guarantee_stall` `merge_admission_terminal_verdict_stall`. FILED AS A STALL AND NOT A RUNG DROP: a 4b(3) drop asserts a REGRESSION and nothing regressed -- GitHub's rule has evaluated reported check runs this way since the ruleset was created, so the capability was never held and a previous rung would be invented. What existed was 4b(1) inflation, corrected by making the live policy representable.
- **an over-budget refusal reports the BUDGET's interrupt point where a reader expects the SUBJECT's cost, so a family of rows sitting at the top of the cost distribution is diagnosed as either pure jitter or pure cost, and neither reading is right** (INVALID STATE: the floor's per-claim cost budget preempts a claim by polling, and the diagnostic prints `interrupt_point=509ms` against a 500ms budget. That figure is a property of the poll and of the budget -- every interrupted row on every run reports approximately whatever ceiling interrupted it -- and the tool says so in its own sentence, which forbids comparing it with the budget or with another row's. From the floor's own observation the refused cost is `> budget`, UNBOUNDED ABOVE. HARM: the shape of the number invites exactly one wrong inference and it is the comfortable one. A 9ms gap on 500ms reads as a 1.8 percent margin, a margin that small is inside any plausible run-to-run spread, and the conclusion that follows blames the runner and clears the corpus. Nothing in the refusal is false; the reader supplies the subtraction. The opposite over-correction is equally available and equally wrong -- reading the refusals as proof that the rows are over budget -- and a filing that names either one alone sends the next owner to tune costs that are not over budget, or to chase runner variance that would be harmless if the rows had headroom. **SPECIMENS: FOUR IDENTITIES, TWO INDEPENDENT PULL REQUESTS, ONE NIGHT, ALL FOUR UNDER `v2.test.emit.*` (jolly-ferret-412 and zesty-lynx-843, gunbc#10141 and gunbc#10189, 2026-09-02/03).** gunbc#10141's floor refused on `v2.test.emit.rust_call_emit.rust_call_fold_closure_emit_holds` against a tree whose diff from its predecessor was whitespace only; the corpus-wide run factor was 0.970, so the refusing run was FASTER than the reference, and 3,511 of 3,527 rows were byte-identical in `eval_steps`. gunbc#10189 then refused on `v2.test.emit.produced_decl_two_target.produced_decl_two_targets_render_own_order`, `v2.test.emit.rust_call_emit.rust_call_fold_closure_swap_discriminates` and `v2.test.emit.rust_logic_meet_join_emit.rust_logic_meet_emit_holds`. Random jitter across 3,524 rows does not land four for four inside one namespace. **THE POLL QUANTUM IS 1,024 EVAL STEPS, AND THE NEGATIVE CONTROL IS WHAT MAKES IT A FINDING RATHER THAN A PATTERN IN FOUR NUMBERS (observed by zesty-lynx-843, who handed it over rather than writing it up).** All four interrupted rows report an exact multiple: 196,608 = 192 x 1024; 176,128 = 172 x 1024; 155,648 = 152 x 1024; 163,840 = 160 x 1024. The SAME identity when it COMPLETED reports 175,664 steps -- 171.5 x 1024, not a multiple -- and reports EXACTLY 175,664 again on a second completing run, which is the determinism the estimate below depends on, measured rather than assumed. A completed row reports its true count; an interrupted row reports a 1024-quantised one. **THAT QUANTISATION MAKES THE FULL COST OF EVERY REFUSED ROW ESTIMABLE, WHICH THE FLOOR ITSELF CANNOT DO.** The tree is byte-identical and the witnesses deterministic, so the step count to completion is the same on every run; interrupted steps over completed steps gives the fraction of the work reached, and the interrupt point over that fraction gives the full cost. All four specimens completed on one later run, so all four are estimable: 196,608/197,227 = 99.7 percent, ~511ms; 176,128/176,624 = 99.7 percent, ~510ms; 155,648/159,355 = 97.7 percent, ~521ms; 163,840/175,664 = 93.3 percent, ~546ms. THREE OF THE FOUR WERE PREEMPTED WITH UNDER 3 PERCENT OF THEIR WORK LEFT -- they did not blow through the budget, they ran out of it at the finish line, which no reading of the refusal alone would show. The floor is right that it holds NO upper bound from its own observation: it was preempted and cannot know. A second observation of the same deterministic witness supplies what one cannot. A separate and smaller quantity, not to be conflated with it: one poll interval is ~2.1ms of work at the green rate, so the overshoot past the ceiling is small and ~509 hides no large excursion. **THE MECHANISM IS THE CONJUNCTION, NOT EITHER HALF, AND THE HEADROOM IS THE MEASURED QUANTITY.** Against pure contention: four for four inside one namespace. Against pure cost: ALL FOUR COMPLETE UNDER BUDGET ON AN ORDINARY RUN, at 353, 381, 387 and 418ms -- 71 to 84 percent of the 500ms budget -- so not one of them is over budget. Their per-step rates on that run span 2.12 to 2.22 microseconds, a 5 percent spread across four rows in three modules, which is the uniformity of one shared dominant producer rather than four independent costs. **THIS ROW CARRIES THREE SUCCESSIVE READINGS OF ITS OWN SPECIMENS, AND THE FIRST TWO ARE KEPT BECAUSE THE SEQUENCE IS THE FINDING.** READING ONE, RETRACTED IN PART: the emit family sits at the top of the cost distribution with 16 to 29 percent headroom and is therefore what any slowdown converts into refusals first, so insufficient headroom is the deficit. READING TWO, RETRACTED IN WHOLE: a join of `required-floor-claim-cost` across one refusing and three green runs found that over the 2,128 identities carrying non-zero cpu in every run, EXACTLY ONE had a four-run maximum above the 500ms budget, that the ten highest-median identities sat at 71 to 84 percent of budget with spreads of 1.13x to 1.38x, and that therefore NONE OF THEM COULD REACH THE LINE -- so proximity was the wrong ranking and spread was the right one. **READING TWO WAS FALSIFIED BY THE FIFTH RUN, AND IT WAS FALSIFIED IN THE MOST DIRECT WAY AVAILABLE: the two rows it named as unable to cross both crossed, on one run, ten days of argument later than the four samples it rested on (gunbc#10304, run 33824873607).** `rust_produced_decl_name_discriminates` -- reading two's own example of a row that cannot reach the line, the corpus's highest median at 84 percent of budget with a 1.13x spread -- was interrupted at cpu_at_least 524ms. `produced_decl_two_targets_render_own_order`, one of this row's four original specimens at a 78 percent median, was interrupted at cpu_at_least 504ms. `produced_decl_module_folds_declarations_in_order`, the third-highest median at 77 percent, completed at 576ms over its requirement. The fourth-highest completed at 495ms, five milliseconds under. **THE ERROR IN READING TWO IS NAMEABLE AND IS THIS ROW'S OWN NEIGHBOUR: A MAXIMUM OVER FOUR DRAWS WAS TREATED AS A BOUND.** `max across four runs` supports only `no sample above X in four draws`, which licenses no claim whatever about what cannot happen; and the same arithmetic that makes an interrupt point a lower bound rather than a cost makes a small-sample maximum a lower bound on the excursion rather than a ceiling on it. Both are censored observations read as exact ones, one level apart. **READING THREE IS THE ONE THE FIVE RUNS SUPPORT, AND IT RESTORES PROXIMITY WITHOUT RESTORING THE HEADROOM MECHANISM.** Pooling every completing row against its own five-run median -- 10,454 observations over 2,098 identities -- the per-row excursion distribution is p50 1.00, p90 1.17, p99 2.00, max 3.00. So excursions of 2x are an ordinary hundredth-percentile event rather than an anomaly, and the budget is reachable from any median above 250ms; 76 identities sit above that line. Proximity ranks them correctly and reading two was wrong to discard it: the three highest medians in the corpus are exactly the three rows that crossed on the fifth run, in rank order, and the fourth missed by 5ms. WHAT DOES NOT SURVIVE FROM READING ONE IS THE MECHANISM. The corpus did NOT inflate on either refusing run: new-run cpu against the median of four baselines is p50 1.00, p75 1.08, p90 1.18 across 2,120 shared identities, and the earlier refusing pair sat at a median 1.04. So the slowdown that tips a row is IDIOSYNCRATIC AND PER-ROW, not a shift of the distribution the row sits in -- three of this row's four original specimens PASSED on the same run that interrupted the other two, at 388, 391 and 399ms. A family is not tipped as a family. **AND BOTH CROSSINGS ARE THEMSELVES CENSORED, WHICH IS THIS ROW'S SUBJECT ARRIVING IN ITS OWN EVIDENCE.** 504 and 524 are `cpu_at_least` on interrupted rows, not costs, and their step counts are 1024-quantised -- 172,032 = 168 x 1024 and 163,840 = 160 x 1024 -- against completing counts of 197,227 and 169,297. Applying this row's own estimator, the two rows reached 87.2 and 96.8 percent of their work, so their full costs were approximately 578ms and 541ms. Quoting 504 and 524 as the amounts by which they exceeded the budget would understate the first by 74ms, and it is exactly the subtraction this row exists to forbid. **THE STATEMENT THAT SURVIVES ALL THREE READINGS, AND THE ONLY ONE ANY OF THEM SHOULD HAVE ASSERTED (bright-ram-778):** the gate takes ONE SAMPLE of a quantity that varies while `eval_steps` proves the underlying work is fixed, and compares it to a fixed budget. Any row whose distribution has mass above the budget therefore blocks someone eventually, and neither proximity nor spread estimated from a handful of runs bounds WHICH row or WHEN. That is a defect in the estimator and no threshold repairs it -- moving the budget moves which 76 rows are exposed. **THE SHARPEST INSTRUMENT AVAILABLE IS A CONTROLLED PAIR, AND IT REFINES READING THREE RATHER THAN OVERTURNING IT (the pair was constructed by calm-eagle against their own change, which is why it exists at all).** Two floor runs on heads ONE COMMENT-ONLY COMMIT APART -- identical executing witness sets, opposite verdicts, runs 33818376576 passing and 33824873607 refusing. That isolates run-to-run variance from every content hypothesis at once, which no number of independent runs can do. Joining them on identity over the 2,069 completing rows whose `eval_steps` are identical in both: the corpus ratio is p50 1.02, p75 1.13, p90 1.29, and 22 percent of rows exceed 1.15. So the earlier statement that the slowdown is IDIOSYNCRATIC AND PER-ROW is too strong, and is corrected here rather than left standing: there IS a small systematic component, and it is COST-DEPENDENT. Stratifying the same pair by baseline cost decile, the cheapest deciles sit at a median ratio of 1.00 while the most expensive decile -- 143 to 425ms -- sits at 1.11 with a p90 of 1.37. The `v2.test.emit` and `v2.test.execution` rows carry a median 1.07 against 1.00 for everything else. **WHICH MEANS PROXIMITY AND INFLATION COMPOUND RATHER THAN COMPETE, AND THAT IS WHY THE TOP OF THE RANKING CROSSES FIRST.** An expensive row is both closer to the budget AND inflated by more when the run is a bad one, so its crossing probability rises faster than proximity alone predicts. That is a partial restoration of reading one's INTUITION -- expensive rows are the exposed ones -- while its stated MECHANISM stays retracted, because the effect is nowhere near large enough to be what tips a row on its own: 1.11 on a 420ms row reaches 466ms, and the observed crossings were 1.31 to 1.53. The bulk of any single crossing is still unexplained per-row excursion. The pair also carries the caveat on its own face: it is ONE pair, its ratios are computed against a single baseline observation rather than a median, and a 1ms-granularity cost field makes the cheap deciles' ratios mostly rounding. It excludes `the corpus is flat and every crossing is idiosyncratic`; it does not establish the size of the systematic term. **ONE MECHANISM WAS PROPOSED WITH A KILLING ARM AND THE ARM FIRED, WHICH IS RECORDED BECAUSE A REFUTED CANDIDATE IS THE PART OF A CAUSE HUNT THAT NORMALLY GOES UNWRITTEN (hypothesis by bright-ram-778, tested here).** The candidate was that inflation at the ceiling is CONCURRENCY-DEPENDENT -- many simultaneous floor runs contending for memory bandwidth and cache would inflate cpu for identical work -- with the prediction, stated before looking, that the refusing runs would be the busy ones. Counting repository runs in flight at the moment each floor job STARTED, against the two rows that crossed: concurrency 42, 44, 38, 26, 23 against cpu 440, 401, 389, 441, 524 for one row and 459, 393, 392, 389, 504 for the other. The refusing run was the QUIETEST of the five and the slowest, and the correlation is NEGATIVE on both rows. The prediction is refuted in sign, so load in this form is excluded rather than merely unsupported. Five points and a proxy that counts repository runs rather than co-resident work are why this excludes a candidate and does not establish its negation. **AND THE TEST WAS WRONG THE FIRST TIME IN TWO WAYS THAT BOTH READ AS RESULTS, WHICH IS WORTH MORE THAN THE ANSWER.** First, a run's `startedAt` is not when the measurement happened: on the refusing run it read 01:58, thirty-nine minutes after the floor job that produced these artifacts had already finished, because the field tracks the LATEST attempt while the artifacts came from the first. Regressing on it silently regresses on the wrong instant. The floor job's own `started_at`, for the attempt that produced the data, is the only correct clock. Second, concurrency measured from a listing window is RIGHT-CENSORED at the window's edge: the newest run has almost no visible peers because peers start after it, so its concurrency is biased toward zero by construction -- the first pass returned exactly 0 for the run of interest and would have been read as a dramatic result. Both errors independently produced the strongest available version of the finding, which is the direction an unguarded instrument always fails in. **THE SEQUENCE IS KEPT IN FULL BECAUSE EACH READING WAS PRODUCED BY A CORRECT PROCEDURE OVER AN INSUFFICIENT SAMPLE, AND THE SAMPLE GREW EACH TIME:** one night's refusals gave reading one, four runs gave reading two, five runs gave reading three, and nothing here establishes that a sixth would not move it again. A row that carried only the current answer would teach that the question was easy. **THE CENSUS BOUNDARY, STATED BECAUSE THE SELECTION IS THE OBVIOUS OBJECTION TO IT (raised by zesty-lynx-843):** these four rows were selected BY HAVING BEEN INTERRUPTED, so every count here -- the four identities, the headroom range, the rate spread -- ranges over rows that failed to finish. Beside them in the same summary line sits `completed_over_cost_requirement`, rows that REACHED a verdict while exceeding their cost requirement, and that population is uncounted here. It is not a footnote: a row drifting upward crosses its requirement BEFORE it crosses the deadline, so the over-requirement rows are the LEADING edge and the interrupted ones the trailing edge, and an interrupted-only census cannot distinguish `more of the same family sitting closer still` from `the whole distribution is drifting up` -- two claims with different repairs. What is recorded rather than claimed: the refusing run carried two such rows alongside its three interrupted ones, and the clean run carried zero alongside zero, so the two edges moved together across the one pair observed, which is a shape and not a series. A SECOND, INDEPENDENTLY MEASURED SPECIMEN OF THAT UNCOUNTED POPULATION arrived from a lane that was measuring something else entirely (merry-deer-84, cross-claim serve versus recompute for `grammar_relation_row_for_emitted`): across four runs the single `completed_over_cost_requirement` row anywhere in that population was `rust_produced_decl_name_discriminates`, at 529ms, and its `eval_steps` were IDENTICAL at 169,297 across all five runs while its cpu ranged 400-529ms. That is the discriminator of this row's own two-column test returning the contention arm on a row the interrupted-only census never sees: constant steps against varying cpu is the machine saying it did the same work, so the figure that crossed the requirement is position-dependent inflation and not the subject's cost. It matters here for one reason beyond corroboration -- the row REACHED a verdict, so nothing about it is censored, and it is therefore the arm of the boundary where the reader's subtraction would have been legitimate and the census still declines to count it. Neither half alone is the cause, and a filing naming only one sends the next owner either to tune costs that are not over budget or to chase runner variance that would be harmless with headroom. **THE TIPPING DOES NOT REPRODUCE, MEASURED BY A DECLARED SINGLE RERUN OF THE IDENTICAL TREE.** That rerun GREENED: the specimen completed at 381ms over 175,664 steps, against an earlier completion at 364ms over the same 175,664 steps. So the refusal is not a property of the tree, and the slowdown that produced it -- roughly 1.4x per step against a corpus that moved 0.970 -- was incidental to that run. AN EARLIER REVISION OF THIS ROW CLAIMED THE OPPOSITE, citing a reproduction that did not exist: run-level endpoints were still serving the FIRST attempt's artifacts and logs, and the identical `cpu_at_least=509ms` looked like agreement precisely because 509 is the budget-and-poll constant this row is about, so it reads the same however many times it is fetched. That is this very class with the subject changed, produced inside its own write-up, and it is recorded rather than quietly corrected. The discipline it cost is worth one sentence: read the TERMINATED job -- `completed_at` non-null on the job itself, and the attempt-scoped endpoint -- never the run-level poll. Two sibling errors with the sign flipped were found the same night on two other instruments, and the class is one: a floor log line matching `::error::` is the guard's own script text echoed in the step's command group, present in a step that SUCCEEDED and never fired it; and a zero-byte log fetch, returned because the tool refuses logs carrying terminal escape sequences, greps identically to a genuinely clean log. Three readings in one night -- a stale result taken as current, an echoed pattern taken as an occurrence, an instrument failure taken as an absence -- all of them `the query returned nothing` standing in for `nothing is there`, and only a positive control separates them. **THE DISCRIMINATING PAIR IS TWO ATTEMPTS OF ONE RUN ID, WHICH IS THE CLEANEST CONTROL THIS CLASS CAN HAVE: nothing differs but the machine and the moment.** RED is run 33713196398 attempt 1, which reports one interrupted-before-verdict row on `rust_call_fold_closure_emit_holds` with cost UNMEASURED. GREEN is run 33713196398 attempt 2 on the same head, which reports zero interrupted rows, zero over their cost requirement, and `verdict=FloorClean` -- the entire emit family reached a verdict. Re-derive from the run and the attempt numbers rather than from any count copied here. gunbc#10189's run carried `completed_over_cost_requirement=2`, `over_cost_line_diagnostic=323` and `withheld_cost_debt=167`, so the family sits inside a large standing cost-debt population. WHAT THE INTERRUPTED ROW ESTABLISHES DIRECTLY is a bound in the other direction: interrupted after 163,840 steps above 500ms, its rate exceeded 3.05 microseconds per step against 2.07 on the completing run. That is an INEQUALITY and a LOWER BOUND -- the interrupt truncates the step count as well as the cost -- and it must never be quoted as a rate. **AN EARLIER DRAFT OF THIS ROW ARGUED A 1.8 PERCENT MARGIN AGAINST A MEASURED RUN-FACTOR SPREAD OVER A TEN-ROW NEAR-CEILING BAND, AND IS RETRACTED HERE RATHER THAN DELETED**, because that retraction is the class: the margin does not exist, and the band was not the population. **THE HARM IS NOT MERELY A WRONG NUMBER, IT IS AN INVERTED RANKING (crisp-ram-568).** A censored value's magnitude is approximately THE CEILING THAT STOPPED IT, which is the largest figure in the artifact -- so reading bounds as costs RANKS DEADLINE-STOPPED ROWS ABOVE GENUINELY EXPENSIVE ONES, and the expensive work hides behind the interrupted work. Every optimisation decision taken from that ranking is aimed at the ceiling rather than at the machine. This row's own specimen shows it: the emit family reads as the top of the cost distribution partly because its interrupted rows all report approximately the same ~509. The review tell is a single column carrying both populations under one name, and a consumer that sums or ranks it with no case split. **THE CLASS HAS A SECOND EMISSION PATH AND IT HAS NOT CLIMBED (crisp-ram-568; one row rather than two, ruled by tidy-swift-334, because 4b(1) makes a class's rung the MINIMUM across its in-scope paths and two rows would let the repaired path report a climb while the unrepaired one sat silent).** The CONSOLE path carries the typed split -- an interrupted arm whose cost field renders `UNMEASURED`, with an executing probe asserting no digit may occupy it. The ARTIFACT path does not: `required_floor_claim_cost.tsv` renders plain wall and cpu millisecond columns for every executed row, interrupted ones included, distinguished only by an adjacent status string and a `verdict_reached` boolean. Verified by execution on one run's two attempts, same identity, same columns: `budget_interrupted false 513 509` beside `pass true 382 381`, where 509 is a LOWER BOUND and 381 is EXACT. That artifact is the one other roster rows name as THE COMPLETE POPULATION, so the silent path is the load-bearing one. AND THE AUTHOR OF THIS ROW CONSUMED IT: the ~546ms estimate above was computed by reading that cpu column for an interrupted row. It is sound only because it is paired with the completed step count and says so -- the artifact neither required that pairing nor would have prevented projecting 509 alone -- which is the strongest available evidence that repairing the console path does not repair the class. A prior repair on that same path is the second piece of evidence: a qualifier answering `at least` versus `exactly` was DELETED because every caller supplied the same cost sentence anyway, a caveat competing with a number in a cost field and losing. **DISTINCT FROM ITS NEIGHBOUR, AND THE DISTINCTION DECIDES THE REPAIR:** `censored_estimator_drops_its_own_tail` is the OPPOSITE OPERATION on the same rows -- there interrupted rows are REMOVED and a statistic over the survivors is reported as a population statistic; here they REMAIN and their lower bounds are PROMOTED to exact point measurements. Dropping the interrupted row to repair this class CREATES that one. The mechanism is already carried by `gunbc.witness_row_cost` `witness_cost_seed_timed_out_event` and is cited rather than restated. That a coefficient cannot recover the value is settled by receipt: one rendered 502ms figure denoted a row whose completed cost was 2,902ms and a row whose was 355ms -- same displayed number, opposite subjects, one nearly six times over the ceiling and one comfortably under it. **RUNG FOUND AT: BELOW THE FLOOR ON THE ARTIFACT PATH -- SILENT WRONGNESS, WHICH IS NOT A RUNG AND IS FORBIDDEN OUTRIGHT -- AND THEREFORE FOR THE CLASS, corrected from an earlier declaration of 1 in this row.** THE NAMING MATTERS BECAUSE TWO DIFFERENT PLACES SIT OFF THE LADDER, one sentence apart in the authority, and only one of them is forbidden. Silent wrongness is below the floor and must be repaired. `Outside the modeled guarantee` -- external reality, undecidable properties, undeclared intent -- is a legitimate declared boundary that may stand indefinitely. A row saying only `outside the ladder` reads as either, and a later reader reaching for the charitable one leaves a forbidden state standing under a citation that appears to authorise it. THIS CLASS IS THE FORBIDDEN ONE: the cost is not external, undecidable, or unstated -- it is MEASURED AND THEN MISREPRESENTED, a fabricated plausible output at the point of consumption. The console path IS mitigatable: the line stops, the diagnostic is typed and located, and it carries the sentence forbidding the misreading — what it cannot do is stop a reader performing the subtraction anyway. The artifact path is not on the ladder at all. Rung 1 requires that harm be CONTAINED, by total operations, typed outcomes, bounds, rollback or isolation; an adjacent advisory boolean beside a plain millisecond column contains nothing — it does not refuse, bound, or prevent, and the row above says in its own words that a consumer may project that column alone. A censored LOWER BOUND rendered where an exact cost is read is a fabricated plausible output at the point of consumption, which this document places below the floor: silent wrongness is not a rung, it is outside the ladder and forbidden outright. Since 4b(1) takes the MINIMUM across in-scope paths, and one path is below the floor, the class is below the floor until the construction lands. **BELOW-FLOOR IS A REPAIR OBLIGATION, NOT A LOWER RESTING PLACE**, and the repair is not an adjacent warning: the artifact path must REFUSE, or must render the value AS THE CENSORED BOUND IT IS. Either kills the fabrication; a boolean beside an unchanged millisecond column kills neither, which is the whole finding. That also splits the obligation in two, and a single trigger usually names only the second half: this class must first REACH the ladder by ceasing to fabricate, and only then climb it. The ceiling and trigger below describe the climb; reaching the ladder is prior to them and is not optional. **THE EARLIER DECLARATION OF 1 WAS RUNG INFLATION INSIDE THE ROW ABOUT RUNG INFLATION, and it is corrected here rather than quietly amended** — raised as review 59240 against gunbc#10203, and the evidence that decided it was this row's own sentence about projecting the column alone. The reported rung must equal the rung established by executed evidence, and `possible to read correctly` is not containment. **CEILING: 4, structurally impossible, corrected from an earlier 3 in this row.** The 3 was argued as `the poll's observation point is a real datum, so the number stays writable` -- which confuses the DATUM with the FIELD. The observation point stays perfectly writable under its own name; what must have no constructor is a BOUND INHABITING A FIELD CONSUMERS READ AS EXACT, and under disjoint wire field names it has none. That earlier reasoning cited the path this row had repaired while the other stayed silent, which is the inflation 4b(1) names, committed inside the row about it. **NEXT TRIGGER, NAMED AS THE WHOLE CAPABILITY AND NOT AS ANY ARTIFACT CONTRIBUTING TO IT: disjoint exact and censored CONSTRUCTORS, AND disjoint wire field names, AND every arithmetic consumer requiring the exact type, AND a mixed population that REFUSES rather than filtering its censored members.** The conjunction is the trigger. A `cost_observation_kind` column standing beside a still-common millisecond field is a better warning and not a climb, and retiring this row on it would be the 4b(3) failure of a trigger naming an artifact that would merely contribute to a capability. A diagnostic reworded to warn harder is not the capability either: the specimen already carried the warning and the misreading happened anyway. **LEG (iii) OF THAT TRIGGER IS A PATH SPLIT TODAY, WHICH IS WHY THE PAIRING IS THE TRIGGER AND NOT ANY LEG OF IT (crisp-ram-568, measured by a probe that came back the wrong way rather than asserted).** On the Rust path it is enforced by construction: the cost reading's only millisecond accessor returns an `Option` with no total accessor beside it, so a caller ignoring the absent arm is refused. On the `.dag` path it is NOT JUDGED -- the declared-type-position carrier declares twelve positions and states as a COUNT that two are wired, and a mismatch at the ELEMENT of a generic container falls in `PositionGenericTypeArgument`, which has no obligation producer. So a list of censored costs passed where exact ones are declared is currently ADMITTED. THE HONEST DESCRIPTION IS NEITHER `the compiler admits a wrong element type` NOR `the wall is absent`: the obligation was never CONSTRUCTED at that position, so it is missing from the census rather than present with a verdict, and that carrier forbids both readings in its own words. Leg (iii)'s next-rung trigger is therefore `PositionGenericTypeArgument` BY NAME -- a producer at that grammar seam building a declared-type obligation -- which is capability-grained and owned by that carrier, not minted here. **THE EXECUTING EVIDENCE KEEPS ITS FOURTH ARM DELIBERATELY INVERTED:** five compiler-fixture arms, of which two are CALIBRATIONS -- an undefined name refuses, and a declared Int receiving a String refuses -- because the checker answers false on a LOOKUP MISS as well as on a refusal, and the draft this model revives lived its whole life green on an invented emitted path for exactly that reason. The exact-type arm compiles; the censored-type arm IS ADMITTED, and that arm goes RED when the producer lands, to be TIGHTENED into a refusal per 4b(4) rather than deleted. **A SECOND NEIGHBOUR, `window_rendered_subject_misattribution`:** the class about which SUBJECT a rendered figure describes. An earlier revision of this row described it by shape and refused to name it, because it did not resolve on main at that revision's landing and a canonical row may cite only what resolves when it lands. It has since landed, so the name is now carried rather than the description. Here the subject is correct and it is the figure's COMPLETENESS that is misread, so the two are not the same class and a repair for either leaves the other standing. **THE REMEDIES ARE NAMED BY THE TOOL AND NOT CHOSEN HERE:** reduce what the witness reaches for, or move it to a lane declaring its own ceiling so the witness reaches a verdict. Which fits the emit family is a decision about the BUDGET's SUBJECT, and a failure-mode filing is not where a subject gets redefined. **AND THE REFUSAL IS DECIDABLE WITHOUT A RERUN, WHICH IS WHAT KEEPS THIS ROW FROM BECOMING A STANDING REQUEST FOR JUDGMENT.** `required-floor-claim-cost` carries `eval_steps` per row, and steps are host-independent and deterministic -- the same identity reports byte-identical counts across separate completing runs. So pair a refused row against a completing baseline: steps AT OR BELOW baseline with materially higher cpu means the row did the same or less work more slowly, which is contention and not the author's; steps ABOVE baseline means the row genuinely does more work, and the diff owns it. The second arm needs no tolerance, because an interrupt can only LOWER the count -- exceeding the baseline despite being cut short is unambiguous. AN EARLIER STATEMENT OF THIS TEST SAID `SAME steps`, WHICH IS UNREACHABLE ON THE POPULATION IT ADJUDICATES: an interrupted row's count is partial by construction, so it can never equal or exceed its completed baseline, and the arm was undecidable exactly where it was needed. **HOW EVERY DERIVED FIGURE IN THIS ROW IS RE-DERIVED, stated because a run id names the inputs and not the arithmetic** (raised as REQUEST_CHANGES by codex, review 59270, against DESIGN.md 6's `name the instrument, never transcribe its output`): the inputs are three columns of `required-floor-claim-cost` -- `wall_ms`, `cpu_ms`, `eval_steps` -- read per identity from the runs named beside each figure. The per-step rate is `cpu_ms / eval_steps` on the SAME row, never across rows. A full-cost estimate is that row's rate multiplied by a completing baseline's `eval_steps` for the same identity, which is why it is an estimate and not a measurement: it assumes the interrupted row would have continued at the rate it had already established, and that assumption is the estimate's entire content. Headroom is `cpu_ms` of the completing baseline against the 500ms budget. The work-remaining percentage is `1 - (interrupted eval_steps / baseline eval_steps)`. Each of those is one arithmetic step from named columns, so a reader with the artifact can reproduce or refute any figure here without asking the author what was done. THE ONE THING THAT CANNOT BE RE-DERIVED BY RERUNNING IS THE REFUSAL ITSELF, and that is not a gap in the citation but the row's finding: the declared single rerun of the identical tree GREENED, so an instrument that reproduced these numbers on demand would be contradicting the observation this row exists to record. A specimen is a dated observation, and the honest citation for one names the artifact that HELD it, not a producer that would regenerate it. Corrected on first execution, by a lane running it (zesty-lynx-843), which is the only reason the defect surfaced at all. First use returned same-or-less work at 1.37 to 1.82x cpu on three rows cut at 97.7 to 99.7 percent of baseline steps -- and it dissolved a question no reviewer could settle, namely whether the diff's paths reached an emit producer, because had they done so the rows would have done MORE work and they did not. READ THE ARTIFACT AND NOT THE JOB LOG: the log carries `fill_eval_steps`, `marginal_eval_steps` and `measured_eval_steps`, whose names CONTAIN the row-level field as a substring and whose values are different quantities, so a grep for `eval_steps=` over the log over-captures by more than thirteen to one and a lane following it compares the wrong column confidently. **THE TEST HAS A THIRD ARM AND IT REFUSES, BECAUSE THE TWO ARMS DO NOT PARTITION THE SPACE (raised by deep-badger-41 as a reading of the instrument, not a result from it).** They are exhaustive on the STEP axis -- steps are totally ordered, so at-or-below versus above leaves no residue there, and a large step deficit is absorbed by the contention arm by construction rather than by default. The gap is that the first arm is a CONJUNCTION: steps at-or-below AND cpu materially higher. A row with steps at-or-below and cpu NOT materially higher matches neither arm -- it did no more work, it was not slower, and it refused anyway. That residue is probably empty, and the reason is what makes the arm cheap: an interrupt establishes cpu above the budget, so `not materially higher` requires a baseline already at the ceiling, which is itself a finding rather than an acquittal. Probably-empty argues for making it REFUSE cheaply, not for leaving it implicit. So: ANYTHING ELSE IS UNCLASSIFIED -- the comparison does not settle that row, and it escalates rather than exonerates. The direction of the lean is the whole argument: an operator holding a two-arm test and an unclassified result files it under the nearest arm, which here is the EXONERATING one, so an incomplete partition produces not confusion but SYSTEMATIC FALSE EXONERATION -- aimed at the rows matching no pattern, which are the interesting ones -- and it conceals itself, because an exonerated row is one nobody looks at again. That is the absorbing fallback at instrument scale, uncertainty resolving toward `not my problem` instead of toward a refusal. AND THE TEST RANGES OVER BOTH NEAR-CEILING POPULATIONS OR IT ANSWERS HALF THE QUESTION: interrupted rows carry `cost=UNMEASURED`, while `completed_over_cost_requirement` rows completed and their cost is genuinely measured, so they are not protected by the no-upper-bound caveat and a census that compares only interrupted sets never covers them.
